消息 12 assistant

外部DeepSeek原记录 thinking
Key: the s0 reference is for a mug ("cup"), but our task is "pick up the book and place it in the back compartment of the caddy". Object names: black_book_1, white_yellow_mug_1. So book is the target.

Now the caddy geometry in this scene: from the high-res agentview image, the caddy spans roughly col 100-610, rows 325-580. Its long axis is world-y (image left-right). It has compartments. The "back compartment" per memory = caddy's local back = the shallow pocket on the NEAR-robot side (smallest |world x|).

Hmm, in the initial image, the robot comes from the near/bottom? Let's check: the agentview camera looks from a position... eef at (-0.309, 0.0036, 1.157). The deep far pockets (which read as the\nintuitive \"back\" in the robot's view) were decoys and never fired.\n\n**Why:** BDDL region sites are defined in the fixture's LOCAL frame. The object's\ncanonical orientation decides which pocket is \"back\"/\"front\"; it is independent of\nwhere the robot sits. A caddy can be placed with its local back toward the robot.\n**How to apply:**\n- Do NOT equate \"back\" with \"far from robot (most negative x)\". Enumerate ALL\n  pockets, get each world xy, and treat the named side as a HYPOTHESIS, not a fact.\n- Use the hold-and-watch probe ([[libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped]]) to\n  test candidate pockets cheaply. If the deep/far pockets fail, TEST THE NEAR one\n  \u2014 even if its name (\"front\") seems to contradict the task word (\"back\").\n- Physical fit is a strong prior on the WRONG target too: here only the deep\n  pockets fit a low seat, which lured 3 attempts into them; the correct near\n  pocket is shallow and the mug just perches \u2014 and that fires.\n- Order probes to cover both local-frame interpretations (near AND far) before\n  concluding a task is kinematically hard.\n**Falsify:** a caddy/tray task where \"back compartment\" reliably maps to the\nfar-from-robot pocket across seeds, i.e. the region is defined in the world/robot\nframe not the fixture frame.\n**Related:** [[libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped]]\n"

Enumerate ALL\\n  pockets, get each world xy, and treat the named side as a HYPOTHESIS, not a fact.\\n- Use the hold-and-watch probe ([[libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped]]) to\\n  test candidate pockets cheaply. If the deep/far pockets fail, TEST THE NEAR one\\n  \\u2014 even if its name (\\\"front\\\") seems to contradict the task word (\\\"back\\\").\\n- Physical fit is a strong prior on the WRONG target too: here only the deep\\n  pockets fit a low seat, which lured 3 attempts into them; the correct near\\n  pocket is shallow and the mug just perches \\u2014 and that fires.\\n- Order probes to cover both local-frame interpretations (near AND far) before\\n  concluding a task is kinematically hard.\\n**Falsify:** a caddy/tray task where \\\"back compartment\\\" reliably maps to the\\nfar-from-robot pocket across seeds, i.e. the region is defined in the world/robot\\nframe not the fixture frame.\\n**Related:** [[libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped]]\\n\"\n}"

The rear cell of that pair = \"back\ncompartment\", the near cell = \"front compartment\". Get the target cell's interior\ncenter with region `back_project` over its dark interior pixels. Confirm the book\nfits at the current wrist yaw; rotate_wrist 90deg only if the held object's long\naxis lies along the cell's short axis.\n\n**Falsify:** a caddy task where \"back compartment\" resolves to a full-depth side\ncell (no front sibling), or where the predicate region is the whole rear row\nrather than the divided pair's rear cell.\n\n**Related:** [[task-family_libero10_swap_t5]]\n"
